# V2.2 Polish Backlog

Running list of refinements, enhancements, and UX adjustments identified during V2.2 migration work. Intended to be addressed in a dedicated polish phase after Phase 7 is complete and the V2.1 code paths are deleted.

Claude Code: update this file as new items are identified during migration work. Do not address any item on this list during the migration itself unless it gates a Phase's acceptance criteria.

Each item includes:
- **Status** — one of `Open` / `Partial` / `Deferred to Phase X` / `Investigation`
- **Effort** — `S` (small, <2h), `M` (medium, 2–6h), `L` (large, 6–12h), `XL` (multi-phase), `?` (unknown)
- **Source** — which phase or conversation surfaced it
- **Depends on** — prerequisites, if any

Item numbers are permanent IDs; they are never resequenced. Gaps (#84, #92, #109) are historical artifacts. Phase 11.5 hygiene pass moved completed items to the bottom-of-file `## Completed` section — open / partial / deferred items live in their topic sections above.

## Visual & Rendering

### 4. Layout density improvements
- **Status:** Open
- **Effort:** L
- **Source:** Phase 2 visual review
- **Context:** Alice's canvas is crowded with Assets, Parse Results, Claims, and Eval Results all on the parent layer. Nodes overlap in edge paths; edge crossings are frequent.
- **Proposed fix:** Evaluate orthogonal edge routing, edge bundling, or tighter node clustering by relationship. May require V2Canvas refactor.

**Child-layer burial rules (Phase 14.3 design notes — Parse Results COMPLETE; Eval/PoE OPEN, gated on #26)**

Now that all six node types exist (Actor, Asset, Parse Result, Claim, Eval Result, PoE) and netgraph mapping is settled per perspective, candidates for child-layer burial:

- **Parse Results** — ✅ **COMPLETE (Phase 20.0 → 20.2).** Parse Results live on the child layer of their source Asset: dive via double-click on an owned Asset with Parse Results (20.0), or click a Parse Result row in the Asset Detail Panel (20.1); the StackBadge tooltip + child-layer entry affordances are polished (20.2). Only connect to their parent Asset; only owned by the Asset owner; no disclosure-edge issues — the cleanest burial case, fully shipped across the Phase 20 arc. See `references/child-layer-design.md` + arch-spec §5.
- **Eval Results + PoEs** — STILL OPEN. Burial candidates under Claim, depending on perspective:
  - From the evaluator's view (e.g., Bob): own Eval Results + PoEs could potentially live in a child layer of the foreign Claim they target.
  - From the Claim owner's view (e.g., Alice): foreign-owned Eval Results + PoEs visit through proof-only DAs; burial under the Claim makes sense IF they're treated as referenced Assets. This depends on #182 (Amend Claim to include PoE as Asset).
- **Cascading Disclosures (#26) is the gating dependency** for Eval/PoE burial — determines how 2nd, 3rd, N-tier nodes of other parties appear on a user's netgraph. Until Cascading Disclosures is designed, Eval/PoE burial-rule design is incomplete.

Phase ordering: Parse Result burial shipped in Phase 20.0 (no inter-party edges → no #26 dependency). Eval/PoE burial remains contingent on Cascading Disclosures (#26), so the burial rules can account for cascaded artifact visibility.

### 56. Keyboard accessibility
- **Status:** Open
- **Effort:** M
- **Source:** Phase 9A.3 preamble — handoff roster.
- **Context:** Flows today assume mouse input — canvas panning, edge clicks, modal chevron pickers, confidence cycling all fire on click without a clear keyboard equivalent. A pass to wire Tab/Arrow/Enter/Escape through each flow (modal traversal order, canvas-focus-ring, chevron picker via arrow keys, confidence cycle via Space) before a broader a11y audit.

### 57. Mobile/responsive (tablet-friendly max)
- **Status:** Open
- **Effort:** L
- **Source:** Phase 9A.3 preamble — handoff roster.
- **Context:** Layout is desktop-first; the canvas and Detail Panel both assume ≥1280px. Tablet is a realistic demo surface (iPad landscape ≈ 1024×768). Phone is out of scope. Audit: canvas pan/zoom ergonomics on touch, Detail Panel width on narrower viewports, modal widths, chrome icon spacing.

## Detail Panels

### 11. Two-tab Overview/Artifact layout for DA and EA Detail Panels
- **Status:** Deferred to Phase 15
- **Effort:** M
- **Source:** Phase 3 visual review
- **Context:** DA and EA Detail Panels currently use a flat layout. Node Detail Panels use Overview + Artifact tabs. Matching the node panel structure would give DA/EA panels a consistent mental model (Overview: parties, subject, scope, terms — Artifact: raw JSON viewer).
- **Proposed fix:** Refactor `DisclosureAgreementDetailPanel.jsx` and `EvaluationAgreementDetailPanel.jsx` to use the same tab structure as node DetailPanels, with a JSON artifact view under the Artifact tab.

### 48. Candidate preview before Request Agreement
- **Status:** Deferred to Phase 15
- **Effort:** M
- **Source:** Phase 7 scope boundary (spec §9)
- **Context:** Spec §9 lists "View a Claim's public-directory Detail Panel (owner, description, posted date, aggregate stats)" as a capability. Currently the candidate card jumps straight to Request Agreement. Add a secondary "Preview" CTA that opens a read-only panel showing what's publicly disclosed about the Claim (respecting the public DA's scope).

### 58. Export JSON (functional) + Export PDF (placeholder)
- **Status:** Open
- **Effort:** M
- **Source:** Phase 9A.3 preamble — handoff roster. **High priority.**
- **Context:** Detail Panels for each artifact type (Asset, Claim, Parse Result, Eval Result, DA, EA) should offer Export actions. JSON export is trivial — serialise the `v22Artifact` (or the underlying artifact) and trigger a browser download; this is functional from day one. PDF export is placeholder-grade (stub button that opens a "coming soon" dialog or generates a minimal PDF via a lightweight library) — the intent is to surface the capability in the UI so client discussions around export formats have a visible hook.
- **Proposed fix:** Add a shared `<ExportActions>` strip to each Detail Panel footer, above the primary action. Two buttons: "Export JSON" (functional) + "Export PDF" (placeholder). Consider a single dropdown if footers get crowded.

### 74. Provenance lineage UI in Detail Panels
- **Status:** Open
- **Effort:** M
- **Source:** Phase 9A.4 — `dot.lineage[]` is populated correctly but not yet visualised. (Rehoused from Process Flows during Phase 11.5 hygiene — this is a Detail Panel surface item.)
- **Context:** Every Asset, Claim, and Eval Result now carries `dot.lineage[]` — an append-only chronological list of state transitions (transfers so far; registration events in a later phase). Surface this in a new "Provenance" section on each Detail Panel: chronological list of entries (timestamp + from DID + to DID + status + optional reason).

### 104. Click-to-jump navigation from Detail Panel association lists
- **Status:** Deferred to Phase 15
- **Effort:** M
- **Source:** Phase 9A.6.1 QA — V2.1 capability lost in V2.2 migration.
- **Context:** V2.1 Detail Panels supported clicking a node listed by association (e.g., a Referenced Asset in a Claim panel, an Eval Result in an Asset panel, etc.) to jump the canvas to that node and select it. V2.2 panels render these lists as static text. Phase 11D.3 wired Eval Result row click in V22ClaimPanel; remaining lists (Parse Results, etc.) deferred. Restore the click-to-jump pattern: each associated-node item becomes a clickable row that calls the existing pan-to-selection helper (`canvasRef.current?.animatedPanToWithZoom`) and sets `sel`. Affects V22NodeDetailPanel and possibly the Agreement panels too.

### 202. Remove "ACTOR" and "ASSET" small-caps labels from Detail Panels
- **Status:** ✅ Complete (Phase 17.3 + Phase 17.3.1, 2026-05-19). Phase 17.3 closed the V22NodeDetailPanel scope: `PanelHeader`'s `typeLabel` prop is now optional; V22ActorPanel + V22AssetPanel omit it. CLAIM / EVAL RESULT / PARSE RESULT / PROOF OF EVALUATION / BADGE TEMPLATE panels keep their type pill since those are abstract artifact concepts. Phase 17.3.1 extended the audit to RfpDetailPanel: removed the "ACTOR" pill from the "POSTED BY" row and the "ASSET" pill from the "FOR ASSET" row. Relational headers (POSTED BY / FOR ASSET) and the "YOU" indicator preserved — they convey relationship, not category. **#202 is now fully closed across all Detail Panel surfaces.**

### 161. Notification deep-link with diff highlighting in Detail Panel
- **Status:** Open
- **Effort:** M
- **Priority:** Medium
- **Source:** Phase 11E.1 QA — UX gap when an amendment notification deep-links to the affected node.
- **Context:** When a user clicks an amendment notification (EA amendment today; DA + Claim amendments once #102 ships), the deep-link pans/selects the target node and opens the Detail Panel — but the panel is undifferentiated from a fresh open. The user still has to hunt for what changed. The flow should make the diff visible: scroll the panel to the changed section (Terms, Acknowledgments, Referenced Assets) and render NEW badges + highlighted rows on the specific added / edited rows.
- **Persistence rule:** Highlighting persists until the Detail Panel is closed, then clears. Reopening the panel from the same notification re-applies the highlight; reopening via a different path (canvas click, agreement edge) does not.
- **Proposed fix:** Notification deep-link payload extended with a `diff` field carrying `{ changedSections: [...], newRowIds: [...], editedRowIds: [...] }`. Detail Panel reads diff state on mount when triggered via notification; applies amber row highlighting + NEW badge to the specified rows; clears on unmount. Cross-cuts EA amendment (live), DA amendment (#102 scope), Claim amendment.
- **Depends on:** Pairs naturally with #102 (reciprocal DA-amendment notifications) — both shape the same notification → Detail Panel UX. May ship together or as #102's polish follow-up.

## Data Model & Content

### 17. Terminology reconciliation with client canon
- **Status:** Open
- **Effort:** M
- **Source:** Andrew's client feedback post-spec-review
- **Context:** Client analysis confirmed the architecture model holds, but noted deviations from their canon terminology. No structural changes required — just naming drift to reconcile.
- **Proposed fix:** After V2.2 stabilizes, update nomenclature (artifact names, field names, UI labels) to match client canon. Then update the architecture spec markdown to reflect the shipped reality. Client re-runs their analysis.

### 36. Option B — view builder pulls disclosed Assets onto grantee canvas
- **Status:** Open
- **Effort:** M
- **Source:** Phase 6.5 #5 — chose Option A (resolve evidence Assets from shared dataset in the eval modal) as the smaller change. Option B is the architecturally consistent counterpart.
- **Context:** Today, counterparty Assets in scope of an active inter-party DA aren't pulled onto the grantee's canvas (only the Claim is, per §6 pull-in semantics). The eval modal works around this by resolving Assets directly from the shared dataset. Bob can't see the in-scope Assets on his canvas — he sees them only inside modals/panels.
- **Proposed fix:** Update `buildViewForActor` to also pull in Assets named in `da.scope.assetIds` for active inter-party DAs where the actor is grantee. Re-evaluate canvas density after; may pair with item #4 (Layout density improvements).

### 53. Session persistence via localStorage
- **Status:** Open
- **Effort:** S
- **Source:** Phase 9A.3 preamble — handoff roster.
- **Context:** Current state (role, selection, provisional artifacts, notifications, in-progress modals) lives entirely in React memory. A page reload wipes everything. Persist the user-facing state slice to localStorage so demo sessions survive accidental refreshes. Be conservative about what's persisted — only what the user would expect to see after returning (role, provisionals, dismissedReqs), not ephemeral UI state (hover, pan/zoom mid-animation).

### 61. Factory audit — flag preservation through `makeX`
- **Status:** Open
- **Effort:** S
- **Source:** Phase 9A.3 preamble — handoff roster. **Medium priority.**
- **Context:** Factory functions (`makeAsset`, `makeClaim`, `makeParseResult`, `makeEvaluationResult`, `makeDisclosureAgreement`, `makeEvaluationAgreement`) should preserve runtime-only flags on passed-in data without dropping them. Known flags: `_isNew`, `_isEdgeEndpoint`, `_edgeEndpointSide`, `_aiOriginalValue`, `confidence`, `_isDeclined`, `_declineMeta`, `_showAsProvisional`. Future additions should similarly round-trip. Audit each factory's output object construction — spread-then-override is the safe pattern; explicitly listing known fields risks dropping new flags silently.

### 82. Parse Result DOT (layer-placement half closed in Phase 20.0)
- **Status:** Investigation (DOT question only; **layer placement = Complete, Phase 20.0**)
- **Effort:** M
- **Priority:** **Design blocker** (DOT)
- **Source:** Phase 9A.5 planning — deferred due to missing architectural decision. Split in Phase 20.0.
- **Context:** This item originally bundled two questions. **(b) Layer placement — RESOLVED (Phase 20.0):** Parse Results now live on the child layer of their source Asset rather than the parent layer (they're always derived, never standalone) — see `references/child-layer-design.md` + arch-spec §5. **(a) DOT — STILL OPEN:** should Parse Results carry a DOT (only Assets, Claims, and Eval Results carry `dot` per spec §2.6)? A DOT would make them first-class identity-anchored artifacts, enabling Parse Result transfer + provenance lineage. Blocked on client decision — DOT semantics touch canon X.1–X.10. The layer-placement resolution does NOT decide the DOT question; the two are orthogonal (a buried child node can still carry or lack a DOT).

### 115. Evaluation Agreement terms checkboxes + metadata schema
- **Status:** Partial (Phase 11C shipped two acknowledgments + expiry; remaining terms list deferred for Andrew's ideation pass)
- **Effort:** M
- **Priority:** Medium
- **Source:** Andrew planning conversation post-9C — making EA terms first-class.
- **Phase 11C ship:** Two acknowledgment booleans on `ea.terms` — `resultConfidentiality` and `attribution` — plus `evaluationDeadline` (defaults to 1 year, demo-only enforcement at evaluation time). Phase 11C.1 architectural correction: terms are responder-authored; the requester acknowledges pre-set commitments the Claim owner authored on the Claim's `acknowledgments[]` field (§10.3). EA carries `acknowledgmentsAccepted: [id, ...]` as an audit trail.
- **Remaining work (Andrew to ideate):**
  - "Permitted Requirements Sets" — multi-select from Bob's proposed list, plus Alice's option to add others
  - "Bob may re-disclose Eval Results to [no one / specific parties / public]" (paired with #141 EA permission gate for proof-only re-disclosure)
  - "Re-evaluation permitted [unlimited / N times / not permitted]"
  - "Self-evaluation by Alice required before Bob's evaluation [yes / no]"
- **Display:** EA Detail Panel shows the granted terms in a readable list. Amend EA modal (#108) lets Alice modify any term mid-flight.
- **Future enhancement:** attach an actual legal document (PDF / structured agreement) to the EA, alongside the structured terms.
- **Depends on:** Pairs with #141, #108. Scope finalization waits on Andrew's ideation pass on the term list.

## Process Flows

### 20. Selective Disclosure: fields vs. assets scope
- **Status:** Open
- **Effort:** S
- **Source:** Phase 4 open question #5
- **Context:** Spec §2.3 says "Selective Disclosure references specific parsed fields." V2.1 also allowed asset-level selection. Current V2.2 implementation is fields-only; Phase 11D.2 documented and wired the field-counts UI for grantees.
- **Proposed fix:** Confirm with client whether asset-level scope is still needed. If yes, add asset selection to Selective disclosure flow. If no, document the constraint as final.

### 55. Error states + edge-case review
- **Status:** Open
- **Effort:** M
- **Source:** Phase 9A.3 preamble — handoff roster.
- **Context:** Each flow has happy-path error messaging but edge cases aren't systematically reviewed: PIN not found, self-PIN rejection, clipboard API failure, localStorage quota exceeded, network-offline (currently unused but will matter once a real backend arrives), modal cancelled mid-submit, role-switch during an open modal, decline/cancel from a revealed notification. Walk each V2.2 flow and surface where the failure mode is silent or ambiguous.

### 72. Extend Transferring to Proofs of Evaluation (PoEs)
- **Status:** Open (scope narrowed Phase 12.2)
- **Effort:** M
- **Source:** Phase 9A.4 — Assets-only scope limitation; rescoped Phase 12.2 to focus on PoE transfer specifically.
- **Context:** Canon X.5 applies uniformly across data elements with DOTs. The UI patterns from Asset transfer (V22TransferAssetModal + notification Accept/Decline + dot.lineage append) port directly to Proofs of Evaluation (#168) once PoEs land as a first-class node type with their own DOT. Claim and individual Eval Result transferring deferred indefinitely — Claims and Eval Results are entangled with their evidence and evaluator lineage in ways that don't translate cleanly to standalone transfer semantics.
- **Depends on:** #168 (PoE node types) — implement after PoEs ship.

### 73. Transfer constraint — Asset backing a disclosed Claim
- **Status:** Open
- **Effort:** S
- **Source:** Phase 9A.4 — known limitation.
- **Context:** Today an Asset can be transferred even if it's the sole evidence backing an active Claim disclosed under an inter-party Disclosure Agreement. The counterparty's view would then show a Claim referencing an Asset that's no longer the Claim owner's to disclose. Options: (a) block the transfer with a hard error, (b) render a warning in the transfer review step listing the affected DAs and require acknowledgment, (c) force the Claim to auto-revise (drop the transferred Asset from `referencedAssetIds[]`) on transfer completion. Client decision needed.

### 75. Transfer timeout
- **Status:** Open
- **Effort:** S
- **Source:** Phase 9A.4 — recipient inaction creates an indefinite pending state.
- **Context:** Demo behaviour: pending transfers stay pending until the recipient accepts / declines or the sender cancels. Real implementation would need a configurable timeout (e.g., 14 days) after which pending transfers auto-decline. Also: a UI cue in the sender's TRANSFERRING badge showing days-until-timeout.

### 80. Accepted-transfer animation sequence on both canvases
- **Status:** Open
- **Effort:** L
- **Source:** Phase 9A.5 planning — deferred. Pairs with #71 and the broader animation-restoration phase.
- **Context:** On transfer accept, the recipient-side reveal (pan-to + NEW badge) works; the sender-side UX is abrupt — the Asset vanishes from the sender's canvas without a retreat animation. Target: choreographed sequence on both canvases — sender canvas shows the Asset fading out with a short "transferred" micro-animation; recipient canvas shows the Asset arriving with a reveal pulse. The Phase 9D.2 unravel primitive provides the leaving-canvas pattern; could be reused here.


### 88. Transfer cascade — Parse Results and dependent Claims on sender side
- **Status:** Open
- **Effort:** M
- **Priority:** **Medium — data integrity**
- **Source:** Phase 9A.5 QA — data integrity concern from 9A.4.
- **Context:** When an Asset transfers out, its Parse Results orphan on the sender's canvas (should transfer with the Asset — they're derivatives per canon). Claims referencing only the transferred Asset are broken (need user decision in the transfer review step — warn / auto-revise to drop reference / block). Related to #73 (transfer constraint on disclosed Claims) but distinct: #73 is about the counterparty's visibility of disclosed Claims post-transfer, #88 is about the sender's own orphaned derivatives and broken Claim references.

### 95. QS picker re-add files preserves custom labels
- **Status:** Investigation (root cause confirmed; fix blocked on V22CreateAssetModal restructure scope)
- **Effort:** S
- **Source:** Phase 9A.6.1 QA.
- **Context:** Root cause confirmed: V22CreateAssetModal's `handlePickerSelect` calls `setRows(newRows)` on every picker return, replacing the entire rows array and losing user-edited labels. Picker-only fix explored (accept `initialSelected` prop to pre-check files on re-open) but insufficient — the modal would still `setRows(newRows)` on return, overwriting labels. Clean fix requires editing V22CreateAssetModal's return handler to merge rows keyed by stable file identity (e.g., `filename + size` or `file.path`). Phase 9E-parallel.2 + Phase 10.1 both excluded V22CreateAssetModal restructure. Pairs naturally with Phase 10.2 hierarchy work where the row-construction path will be revisited.

### 105. Run Evaluation modal: empty-evidence copy update
- **Status:** Deferred to Phase 12
- **Effort:** S
- **Priority:** Medium
- **Source:** Phase 9A.6.1 QA.
- **Context:** When a Claim has no evidence (no referenced Assets or no parseable content), the Run Evaluation modal's evidence pane shows a generic empty state. Split by role: owner: "There is no evidence associated with this Claim. Add evidence to self-evaluate."; non-owner: "There is no evidence associated with this Claim. Ask the owner of this Claim to add evidence to evaluate." Surfaces the right next step.

### 106. Remove evidence picker from Run Evaluation modal
- **Status:** Deferred to Phase 12
- **Effort:** M
- **Priority:** Medium
- **Source:** Phase 9A.6.1 QA — larger design question.
- **Context:** Evaluations are Claim-level, not Asset-level. Bob evaluates the Claim against requirements; all in-scope evidence is automatically included. When Alice amends the Claim (adds/removes Assets), Bob's evaluation is marked stale and re-runs against ALL evidence — no partial/selective combinations. Proposal: remove the evidence picker from V22RunEvaluationModal entirely; the modal becomes a review-rows-only surface. Pairs with #88 (transfer cascade) — both deal with Claim-vs-evidence boundary semantics and should be scoped together.

### 117. Re-Run Evaluation: permissive Asset selection with audit metadata
- **Status:** Deferred to Phase 12
- **Effort:** M
- **Priority:** High
- **Source:** Phase 9C QA observation, reversed mid-chat from the original locked-Assets framing to a permissive framing.
- **Context:** When Bob clicks "Re-Run Evaluation" on a Claim that has been amended (new Assets added, or existing Assets removed), the Asset selection step should be fully permissive — Bob can freely include, exclude, or add to the set of Assets being evaluated, including previously-evaluated ones. No locking. No hard rules preventing particular selections. This respects Bob's autonomy as evaluator: the platform's job is to record what was evaluated, not to enforce what *must* be evaluated. The originally-evaluated Asset set is pre-populated as the default selection, but it's a default, not a constraint.
- **Audit behavior:** Any deviation from the originally-evaluated Asset set is recorded in the new Eval Result's metadata — which Assets were added to this run, which were dropped, which carried over unchanged. Preserves evaluation traceability without constraining Bob's choices. Surfaced in the Eval Result Detail Panel under a "Changes from prior evaluation" section when applicable.
- **NEW badges:** Freshly disclosed Assets (since last evaluation) still carry a NEW badge in the Asset selection step, purely as an informational cue so Bob notices "there are new Assets available since I last evaluated." The badge is non-enforcing — Bob can still skip those Assets if he chooses.
- **Proposed fix:** V22RunEvaluationModal's evidence selection step — pre-populate with the previously-evaluated set as default selection. Render NEW badges on freshly-disclosed Assets. Allow full freedom to toggle any selection. On submit, diff the final selection against the previous evaluated set, store the diff in the new Eval Result's metadata. Surface the diff in the Eval Result Detail Panel.
- **Note on prior framing:** An earlier backlog draft framed previously-evaluated Assets as locked (non-removable in re-run), mirroring the AmendClaimModal lock where Alice can't remove evaluated Assets from her own Claim. That framing was reversed. The asymmetry is intentional: Alice locking evaluated Assets on her side preserves the integrity of evaluations Bob has already made; Bob's own re-run is a fresh evaluation event — he decides its scope.
- **Depends on:** Pairs with #106 (evidence picker removal). If #106 ships first and evaluation becomes Claim-level rather than Asset-picker-level, #117 reshapes around surfacing the diff in the review UI rather than via an explicit picker.

### 141. EA permission gate for proof-only re-disclosure (default-allow today)
- **Status:** Open
- **Effort:** M
- **Priority:** Future
- **Source:** Phase 11D.2 scoping — flagged as out-of-scope future polish.
- **Context:** Today, when a Claim is disclosed Selectively, the grantee can run an evaluation against the disclosed fields under a paired EA without any explicit permission from the grantor about whether the grantee can later re-disclose the resulting Eval Result via Proof-Only to a third party. The current model defaults to allow — POE DAs flow naturally as a Disclosure type. A future iteration may want to gate this at EA terms time (a `terms.allowProofOnlyRedisclosure` boolean the grantor authors during response, similar to other responder-authored terms per §10.5). Pairs with #115 (terms metadata schema growth) and §11.6a (warm-path EA-only response flow).
- **Proposed fix:** Add `terms.allowProofOnlyRedisclosure` to EA terms schema (default true to preserve current behavior). Surface as a checkbox in `CombinedResponseModal` step 3 alongside expiry. When false, suppress Proof-Only entries in the grantee's options when they later attempt to share their Eval Result.

### 160. Production: Option C acknowledgment audit semantics for EAs
- **Status:** Investigation
- **Effort:** L
- **Priority:** Future (production data layer)
- **Source:** Phase 11E.1 scoping — out-of-scope until production data layer is built.
- **Context:** Phase 11E.1 (#108) shipped Amend EA with **Option B** semantics: acknowledgments live on the underlying Claim, and editing them via Amend EA mutates the Claim directly. The EA's `acknowledgmentsAccepted` audit-trail field preserves what the Evaluator originally accepted, but doesn't carry a snapshot of the acknowledgment text itself. Two known limitations: (1) **Multi-EA implicit propagation** — when a Claim has Evaluation Agreements to multiple grantees, editing acknowledgments via one EA changes effective terms for all grantees, but only the targeted EA's grantee receives a `v22-ea-amendment` notification. (2) **Lineage chaining** — each amendment record stores `termsBefore.evaluationDeadline` for diffing against the *current* EA terms; older amendments don't chain through.
- **Proposed fix (Option C, production target):** Snapshot acknowledgments per-EA (`acknowledgmentsAtTimeOfRequest: [{ id, title, description }]` separate from the live Claim state). Multi-grantee notification fan-out when any current grantee's snapshot is amended. Chain `termsBefore` through prior amendments so the diff display is correct for older entries. Migration: snapshot the current Claim's acknowledgments onto each existing EA at migration time; subsequent amendments operate on the snapshot.
- **Architecture spec coverage:** §11.2a documents the full Option B vs Option C shape.

### 206. Solicitation modal submit lag
- **Status:** Open
- **Effort:** S
- **Source:** Phase 18.3 QA (filed Phase 18.3.0.1, Item 3).
- **Context:** Slight perceived lag after clicking "Submit Solicitation" before the modal closes. Likely from the synchronous `setV22Provisionals` (DA push) → `mergeProvisionals` recompute → Directory cluster rebuild firing on the same tick as the solicitation push + the modal close.
- **Proposed fix:** Profile to confirm. If confirmed, candidates are (a) defer the cluster rebuild via `requestAnimationFrame`, (b) close the modal first then push state, or (c) batch the two setStates so React only re-renders once.

### Phase 15 Closing Sweep

End-of-Phase-15 backlog hygiene. Ten items surfaced during the 15.0 → 15.6 work — three from the PDF annotation arc itself, three from the Phase 15.1.1 QA badge polish trio (queued for Phase 14.6 backtrack), four architectural-backlog items from the client meeting. Filed at the close of Phase 15 so the next thread starts with a current queue.

### 183. Legacy /public PDF cleanup
- **Status:** Open — Phase 14.6.1 investigation complete; trim deferred.
- **Effort:** S
- **Priority:** Low
- **Source:** Phase 15.2 ship notes; carried through 15.3-15.6 + 14.6.1 untouched at the file level.
- **Context:** Four legacy `/public/*.pdf` files are still referenced by active V2.2 seed Assets but are not regenerated by `scripts/generate-seed-pdfs.mjs`: `emishielding-datasheet.pdf`, `prm-3a-ic-datasheet.pdf`, `prm-3a-ic-qualification-report.pdf`, `voltage-reference-ic-datasheet.pdf`. They survived the 15.2 cleanup because removing them would silently break the parent Assets' previews. The unbranded random-datasheet visual style breaks the MicroCo-branded narrative the rest of the seed PDFs establish.
- **Phase 14.6.1 investigation outcome (path B was attempted):** All four parent Assets have substantial downstream chains in `v2_2Data.js`:
  - `aEmiDatasheet` → `cEmi` Claim (only Asset on it), `prEmiDatasheet` Parse Result, `daAlicePublicEmi` + `daAliceToCarolEmi` DAs, `erCarolEmi` Eval Result.
  - `dPrmIcDatasheet` + `dPrmIcTestReport` → ChipCo PRM-IC Assembly Claim's `referencedAssetIds` (both Assets), `daveOwnAssets`, ChipCo→Bob disclosure DA scope, parse result, granteeAssetId on a DA.
  - `dVrefDatasheet` → ChipCo Voltage Reference IC Claim's `referencedAssetIds` (only Asset), `daveOwnAssets`.
  Per the brief's "STOP and surface" rule for non-orphan Assets, the trim was deferred without code changes — Andrew picks per-Asset between (a) keep the unbranded PDF, (b) cascade-trim the dependency chain (would destroy the Alice→Carol→EMI demo arc, ChipCo PRM-IC Assembly demo, ChipCo voltage-reference demo), or (c) substitute a generated MicroCo / ChipCo-branded PDF via additions to `scripts/generate-seed-pdfs.mjs`.
- **Two paths:**
  - (a) Migrate parent Assets onto generator-produced PDFs with MicroCo / ChipCo branding (additional spec entries in `scripts/generate-seed-pdfs.mjs` + corresponding `localPath` updates in `v2_2Data.js`). Recommended given the demo-narrative cost of (b).
  - (b) Trim selectively per Andrew's call (each Asset removal cascades to its Claim + downstream artifacts).
- **Composition:** Path (a) is cleaner; path (b) requires a per-Asset decision tree from Andrew.

### 185. Asset-intrinsic anchor mechanism
- **Status:** Open
- **Effort:** L
- **Priority:** Architectural backlog (not yet phased)
- **Source:** Phase 15.6 (#172 closing scope) demo trick. The trick stamps anchors on prior Eval Result rows that reference Assets outside `evidenceUsed[]` so the auto-fill mechanism can populate previously-MISSING rows from newly-attached evidence. In production, anchors and discoveredValues should travel with the Asset, not with the prior Eval Result row.
- **Context:** Production design — when an Asset is registered (or re-evaluated by AI), its annotated values + extraction coordinates live as Asset metadata. Any future Eval Result that picks up the Asset can discover its annotated values without seed-side pre-stamping. Generalizes the Phase 15.6 auto-fill so the trick becomes a real mechanism: "evaluator looks at newly-attached Asset, AI extracts annotated values from the Asset's intrinsic anchor metadata, populates the eval row."
- **Scope:**
  - Schema: an `Asset.anchors[]` field carrying `{ requirementId, page, x, y, w, h, discoveredValue, confidence }` per anchor (or a separate Anchor artifact referenced by the Asset).
  - Re-run carry-forward updated to read anchors from the new Asset (Asset-intrinsic) rather than from the prior Eval Result row.
  - Fresh-eval auto-population: a brand-new evaluation against an RS can pre-populate rows from Asset-intrinsic anchors too, not just re-runs.
  - Anchor authoring path — currently anchors only enter the system via seed; production needs an evaluator-side authoring flow OR an AI-extraction pipeline that emits anchors.
- **Composition:** Production complement to the Phase 15.6 auto-fill. The seed-side demo trick can be retired once this mechanism lands.

### 186. RS template canonicalization
- **Status:** Open
- **Effort:** M
- **Priority:** Architectural backlog (not yet phased)
- **Source:** Phase 15.5 — surfaced when adding new requirements (req-006 SEL immunity, req-007 Burn-in qualification) to the VReg evaluation. Documented as "Prototype shortcut — RS template structure" callout in `architecture-spec.md` §8.6.
- **Context:** The prototype's RS artifact is reference-only — `{ id, name, version }` triple. Requirement definitions (req-001, req-002, …) materialize only as result rows on each Eval Result that uses the RS. There is no canonical RS template enforcing a fixed requirement enumeration; each Eval Result authors its own row list, and seed authoring is the only "evaluator" that does this consistently. This means new requirement definitions can't be added to a published RS and propagated — every consuming Eval Result has to be rewritten.
- **Scope:**
  - Schema: an RS carries a canonical `requirements[]` list `[{ id, label, description, severity, … }]`.
  - Eval Results project against the RS template (each row keyed back to a canonical `requirementId`), not author rows from scratch.
  - Library Tab 2 (Requirement Sets) gains a requirement-list editor for owned RSes.
  - New-version flow extends to changing the requirements list (add/remove/edit rows; bumping version).
  - Migration path for existing seeded Eval Results — initially they continue working via projection-from-rows fallback, eventually they migrate.
- **Composition:** Independent of #185 (Asset-intrinsic anchors) — the two features compose but neither blocks the other. Likely earlier than #185 since the RS template change is more contained.

Both surfaces (Library right-panel and the forward-looking Detail Panel) render consistently.

### 188. Phase 14.6 — Item F: Globe icon on own-RS rows that are also Published Standards — DONE in Phase 14.6 (2026-05-07)
- **Status:** Completed in Phase 14.6 (backtrack ship; scope corrected).
- **Resolution:** Scope was corrected during planning — the Published Standards section already exists in the picker (lines 547-561 of BadgesPanel.jsx). The actual gap was that own-RS rows that the actor authored AND published rendered without any globe-published indicator (because the `externalPublished` filter excludes own-published copies from the dedicated section). Fix: `EditorForm` builds a `publishedRsIdSet` membership Set; `renderRsRow` consults it for own-section rows in addition to the existing `fromSection === 'published'` path. Bob (the only role authoring Published Standards in seed) now sees the globe icon on his own published RS rows in the YOUR REQUIREMENTS SETS section.

### 189. Phase 14.6 — Item G: Issuance enforcement gating (RS-coverage) — DONE in Phase 14.6 (2026-05-07)
- **Status:** Completed in Phase 14.6 (backtrack ship).
- **Resolution:** RS-coverage gate at IssueBadgeModal's template picker. Each Badge Template row computes `disabledReason` from `template.referencedRequirementsSetIds` minus `coveredRsIds` (Set unioned from active PoEs' wrapped Eval Result `requirementsSets[].id` on the target Claim). Failing rows render greyed-out (opacity 0.45, not-allowed cursor, no hover state) wrapped in a Tooltip listing missing RS names; SUGGESTED label suppressed. Two failure shapes: "no PoE at all" → generic "no Proof of Evaluation" tooltip; partial coverage → list missing RS names. `handleV22IssueBadge` re-checks the gate at the data layer and silently rejects with a console warning when bypassed (defense-in-depth). `mergeProvisionals(seed, v22Provisionals)` honored so PoEs created during the session count toward coverage. Self-issuance gating + Phase 14.3 picker scope (own templates only) remain authoritative for those gates. RS-lineage matching is NOT used — badges reference frozen RS versions, exact id match.

### 190. Item H: Badges become netgraph nodes
- **Status:** Open
- **Effort:** XL
- **Priority:** Architectural backlog
- **Source:** Client meeting (filed during Phase 15 closing sweep).
- **Context:** Currently Badges render as chips on Claim node cards (Phase 14.2-14.5), not as first-class netgraph nodes. Treating Badges as nodes would let them participate in the network model — Badges become navigable entities with their own Detail Panels, edges to issuer/recipient/Claim, and disclosure semantics distinct from the parent Claim's.
- **Scope:**
  - Add a Badge node type to V2Canvas + AssetNode dispatcher.
  - Layout column for Badges (probably alongside or downstream of Claims).
  - Edges: Badge → issued-by (issuer Asset), Badge → endorses (Claim), Badge → received-by (recipient Asset).
  - Disclosure: when a Claim with badges is disclosed under SDA, the badges should follow with appropriate scope (maybe under a new badge-scope flag).
  - Migrate existing chip rendering to a hybrid: chip on Claim card stays as a summary affordance, but full-detail navigation goes to the Badge node.
- **Composition:** Composes with #4 / #130 (netgraph cleanup) and #26 (Cascading Disclosures) — those need to land before this can build cleanly. If pursued, Badges become navigable network entities with their own Detail Panels, edges, and disclosure semantics.

### 191. Item I: Custom RS version number at creation time
- **Status:** Open
- **Effort:** S
- **Priority:** Architectural backlog
- **Source:** Client meeting (filed during Phase 15 closing sweep).
- **Context:** Current RS creation flow always assigns `version: 1` automatically. Some scenarios need a custom version (migrating an existing standard with non-1 starting version, intentional version-skipping for legal/contractual alignment, mirroring an external standard's version).
- **Scope:** Add an optional version field to the RS create form. Default to 1; user can override. Validation: positive integer. Subsequent new-version increments operate on the user-specified base.
- **Composition:** Standalone within the Library RS creation flow.

### 192. Item J: RFP / Public Directory request flow with self-evaluation
- **Status:** Open
- **Effort:** XL
- **Priority:** Architectural backlog
- **Source:** Client meeting (filed during Phase 15 closing sweep). Major addition to the Directory Layer.
- **Context:** The Public Directory currently surfaces actors and their published standards (Phase 7 + #43 / #45). Add an RFP (Request For Proposal) flow where:
  - A buyer can post a request specifying the required standards + delivery criteria.
  - Suppliers can respond with self-evaluations against the RFP's required standards.
  - The buyer reviews responses, can initiate a deeper evaluation against any responding supplier's evaluation.
- **Scope:**
  - New RFP artifact type (network-public, owned by the buyer party).
  - Self-evaluation flow on the supplier side — supplier picks the RFP, runs evaluation against its standards, submits as a response.
  - Buyer-side review surface (RFP Detail Panel showing responses with summary metrics).
  - Buyer-initiated formal evaluation flow against a chosen response (re-evaluating the supplier's self-evaluation against the same standards).
- **Composition:** Major addition to the Directory Layer. Composes with #43, #45, #47 (Clickable Directory Layer dots), #132 (Public Directory navigation), #162 / #163 (Directory Layer fixes). Probably one of the larger features in the post-Phase-16 roadmap.
- **Progress:** The buyer-side lifecycle + solicitation loop shipped across Phases 17.0–17.2.1.2 (post → discover → solicit → reject/accept → formalize EA+DA). The **creation flow** (post a new RFP from an Asset) is now closed: entry points in Phase 17.5, two-step `RfpCreationModal` in **Phase 17.5.1** (Submit → `makeRfp` → `v22CreatedRfps` → appears in the owner's Directory cluster via `mergeCreatedRfps`). Remaining open scope: supplier self-evaluation response flow, buyer-side response-review metrics, buyer-initiated formal re-evaluation of a response, plus post-creation orchestration (17.5.2) and edit / withdraw / amend.

### 196. Force-directed cluster layout for scale
- **Status:** ✅ Closed (Phase 16.2.3) — superseded by the polar Poisson disc fan-out. With the bounded 17280×11170 canvas (16" MBP at 10% zoom) as the design surface, each non-active cluster picks (θ, r) via Poisson disc sampling — θ ∈ [-75°, +75°] from straight up, r ∈ [2000, ~8377]. Sort input by descending Claim count so jumbo clusters get first pick. Up to 50 retries per cluster with seed-perturbed samples; collision check via the existing 12-cell buffer; canvas-bounds check. Yields a clean radial fan-out across the upper hemisphere of the canvas for the current 14-cluster scale (12 mock suppliers + ChipCo + MicroCo on Bob's view) without needing force simulation. If future scale demands force-directed (e.g., 50+ clusters where Poisson disc retries hit the 50-attempt cap consistently), refile a successor item.

### 195. Three.js edge migration for Directory Layer
- **Status:** Open
- **Effort:** M
- **Priority:** Low
- **Source:** Phase 16.1.0 deferred scope. The dot matrix grid + Claim/RFP dots migrated to Three.js, but umbrella DA edges + label pillboxes + Actor squares + tooltip card stayed HTML/SVG to preserve the Phase 16.0.3 visual treatment (curved cubic Bezier edge + pillbox-styled labels + parent-layer-AssetNodeDot tooltip pattern with viewport-edge flip).
- **Context:** Migrating edges to Three.js Line2 (matching V2Canvas's edge primitive) would let the umbrella edge participate in the same depth/alpha/render-order pipeline as the dots. Tradeoff: Three.js Line2 doesn't support curved paths natively (only line segments), so the cubic Bezier curve from 16.0.3 would need to either decompose into line segments at sample points, or migrate to a different curve primitive (e.g. CatmullRomCurve3 → tube geometry → line). The HTML/SVG approach is simpler and the curve renders crisply.
- **Composition:** Composes with future Phase 16.x scaling work — if seed grows to hundreds of edges, the HTML SVG approach may stutter and Three.js Line2 with batched draw calls becomes more attractive.

### 193. V2App.jsx file split for transpile-perf + maintainability
- **Status:** Open
- **Effort:** M
- **Priority:** Low
- **Source:** Babel 500KB warning surfaced during Phase 14.6.1 ship; main bundle is ~1.3MB before gzip and `V2App.jsx` is the dominant module by line count (~8500 lines). Composes with #50 (V2.1 dead-code sweep).
- **Context:** `V2App.jsx` carries the parent-canvas chrome, all modal state machines, all V2.2 handler functions, the Detail Panel mount paths, and the Directory Layer mount. The combined size makes Vite's transform pass slow and Babel emits warnings. A split into thematic sub-modules (e.g. `V2AppHandlers.jsx`, `V2AppChrome.jsx`, `V2AppModalRoot.jsx`) would reduce per-module compilation cost and improve readability. Holds until either build perf becomes user-visible or a future maintainability ask justifies the churn.

### Phase 11.6 cleanup queue

End-of-Phase-11 architectural cleanup batch — items surfaced by Phase 11E QA that need to land before Phase 12 starts. Distinct from Phase 14's Directory Layer batch and Phase 15's broader UX polish; this queue is for issues that exposed gaps in the Phase 11 architecture itself. **Phase 11.6 shipped 2026-05-03 (#164 + #165 + #166); items moved to Completed.**

### 167. DA amendment-as-proposal model (extension of #164 to disclosure agreements)
- **Status:** Open (deferred)
- **Effort:** M
- **Source:** Phase 11.6 (#164) deliberate scope limit. The acknowledgment-injection exploit closed by #164's amendment-as-proposal flow is specific to acknowledgments — DA amendments only edit scope + expiration, neither of which carries the same exploit risk.
- **Context:** Even though scope and expiration changes don't carry the same consent-injection risk as acknowledgment changes, the long-term symmetry is desirable. A grantor who adds a previously-out-of-scope Asset to a Selective Disclosure scope post-evaluation could surface evidence the grantee never consented to access, even if the grantee originally only saw fields. Closer audit needed before deciding scope of the proposal/accept gate (e.g., scope-narrowing is always safe; scope-widening may need consent).
- **Proposed fix:** Extend `proposeAgreementAmendment` / `accept…` / `reject…` factory pattern to DA amendments. New EA-style `pending-acceptance` lifecycle on DAs. Reuse `AmendmentResponseModal` with a scope-diff display section instead of (or alongside) acknowledgments. Three new notification types: `v22-da-amendment-proposal` / `v22-da-amendment-accepted` / `v22-da-amendment-rejected` (replacing the unilateral `v22-da-amendment` from Phase 11E.2). Architecture spec §11.2 update mirroring the §11.2a Phase 11.6 revision.
- **Out of scope:** Phase 11.6 shipped EA-only. DA amendments stay unilateral until this item lands.

- **Status:** ✅ Complete (Phase 13). Shipped as a first-class artifact type with `makePoE` factory, retroactive seed migration (every existing seed Eval Result wrapped in a PoE), canvas adapter (`poeToNode` + `COL_OWN_POE` column + wrap edges + counterparty pull-in placement), `PROOF OF EVALUATION` type label + "Wraps N · X SAT · Y UNSAT" aggregate row in AssetNode card body, V22PoEPanel Detail Panel section (Owner / Source Claim / Wrapped Eval Results / Disclosures / Badges placeholder / DOT), `CreatePoEModal` confirmation modal, action-bar + Detail Panel footer entry points, Run Evaluation submit-time termination gate, proof-only DA migration (`scope.evaluationResultIds` → `scope.poeIds` global rename across 6 files), AmendDisclosureModal + CombinedResponseModal proof-only pickers switched to PoE candidates, share-PoE-shares-all grantee canvas pull-in. **Resolved design questions:** PoEs sit in their own column right of own Eval Results (counterparty PoEs hang next to source Claim); share-PoE-shares-all (no per-Eval-Result selectivity); retroactive migration applies to all existing seed Eval Results including solos.

### 169. Badges — DONE in Phases 14.0 + 14.1 (2026-05-06)
- **Status:** Complete (Phase 14.0 shipped templates; Phase 14.1 shipped issuance + display surfaces).
- **Effort:** L (delivered in two parts).
- **Priority:** High (Phase 14 priority #1)
- **Source:** Phase 12 design huddle.
- **Context:** Badges are third-party issued endorsements that reference Proofs of Evaluation (#168). Live as discoverable artifacts on the platform. Surface on both Claim Detail Panels (showing the badge attached to the underlying PoE) and Actor Detail Panels (aggregating all badges across the Actor's Claims).
- **Design decisions (locked in Phase 12.2):**
  - Issuance: any Actor can issue a Badge to any other Actor (not to themselves) — the platform treats all Actors symmetrically. No special "issuer" role; no badge-issuer authority gating.
  - Reference: Badges reference a specific PoE (#168), not a Claim or Actor directly. The Claim and Actor relationships derive from the PoE's lineage.
  - Display: Both Claim Detail Panels (badge attached to the underlying PoE) and Actor Detail Panels (aggregated across all of the Actor's Claims).
  - Revocation: follows existing revocation patterns (Phase 9D unravel + cross-role notification).
- **Open design questions:**
  - Issuance flow UX — modal triggered from the PoE Detail Panel? From the Actor Detail Panel?
  - Badge taxonomy — custom labels only, structured types, or both?
  - Public Directory surfacing — do badges appear on Directory dots, on Claim previews, on Actor cluster headers?
  - Revocation cascade — what happens to the Badge when the underlying PoE is superseded or revoked?
- **Depends on:** #168 (PoE node types).

### 170. Consolidate `assetReferences[]` and `referencedAssetIds[]` on Claims
- **Status:** Open
- **Effort:** M
- **Priority:** Low (tech debt — Phase 12.3 audit trail)
- **Source:** Phase 12.3 Bug B fix.
- **Context:** Phase 12.2 (#122) introduced the `assetReferences[]` audit chain as a parallel field alongside the existing primitive `referencedAssetIds[]` (the active in-scope set). This dual-shape model was a deliberate Phase 12.2 deviation to avoid touching 42+ consumer call sites that read `referencedAssetIds`. Phase 12.3's Bug B fix (`deriveAgreementEdges` and Run Evaluation `scopeAssetIds` not honoring removed entries) was a direct consequence — `da.scope.assetIds` is its own primitive list that doesn't auto-track the active set. The cleanest long-term shape is to make `assetReferences[]` the single source of truth and either (a) drop `referencedAssetIds[]` and migrate all 42+ readers, or (b) keep `referencedAssetIds[]` as a derived-on-read view but enforce derivation through a single helper (already shipped as `getInScopeAssets` in Phase 12.3). Option (b) is the lower-risk path; option (a) closes the door on this entire bug class.
- **Proposed work:** Audit pass — walk every read of `claim.referencedAssetIds[]` and decide whether the consumer wants the active set (most do) or the full audit chain (a few — like AmendClaim's existing-references display). Route the active-set readers through `getInScopeAssets` or its id-only sibling. The `da.scope.assetIds` field on internal claim-ref DAs is a separate primitive that needs its own filter-on-read story — likely a `getInScopeScopeAssetIds(da, claim)` helper.

### 172. PDF annotation overlay: numbered evidence dots
- **Status:** Phase 15.0 (part 1) + 15.0.1 (hotfix) + 15.1 (part 2) shipped. PDF.js integration + seed PDFs + annotation visual redesign + bidirectional row↔dot interaction + side-by-side Expand modal layout + multi-Asset auto-flip. **Phase 15.2 next:** walkthrough markdown guide expansion + legacy /public PDF cleanup + any final polish.
- **Effort:** L-XL (depends on whether #41 PDF.js integration is bundled or scoped separately)
- **Priority:** High (above general polish, below the locked Phase 13+ priority queue; not inserted into the strict ordering yet)
- **Source:** Phase 12.4 design discussion.
- **Context:** During the AI evaluation review step, render numbered dots over the source PDF that anchor each evaluation row to the specific evidence the AI Evaluator used. Hovering a dot shows a tooltip with the excerpt + linked review row. Hovering a review row highlights the corresponding dot(s). This turns the review experience from "you can read the AI's values" into "you can see why those values are trustworthy."
- **Technical dependencies:**
  - Depends on #41 (PDF.js integration). The current iframe-based viewer cannot host overlays — iframe content lives in a sandboxed browser context where DOM elements can't reliably position over page coordinates. PDF.js renders pages as canvas/SVG inside the host DOM, which makes overlay anchoring possible.
  - Data model change: Eval Result rows + Parse Result fields need an `evidenceAnchors[]` field per row. Shape: `{ assetId, page: number, x: number (0-1 normalized), y: number (0-1 normalized), excerpt: string }`.
  - Demo seed authoring is real work — coordinates have to be picked by hand against real PDFs for each demo Eval Result that should show annotations.
- **Scope:**
  - Numbered dots positioned at evidence locations within the PDF (one or more per row).
  - Hover tooltip on each dot showing the excerpt.
  - Bidirectional highlight: hover review row → highlight corresponding dots; hover dot → highlight corresponding row.
  - Page navigation when an evidence anchor lives on a different page than the currently visible one.
- **End-of-Phase-12 assessment criteria:**
  - (a) Sequence vs. priority queue — does this run before or after #168 (PoE) and #169 (Badges)?
  - (b) Bundling vs. independent ship — is #41 (PDF.js integration) bundled into this phase, or split into a prerequisite phase?
  - (c) PCN demand signal — has the client specifically requested this capability, or is it speculative client-impressing scope?
  - (d) Realistic scoping — confirm L-XL estimate after a design huddle on the dot-rendering primitive + tooltip + bidirectional hover plumbing.
- **Depends on:** #41 (PDF.js integration).

### 181. User-uploaded Badge Template graphics
- **Status:** Open — deferred future feature
- **Effort:** M
- **Priority:** Low
- **Source:** Phase 14.0 (#169 part 1) — filed as part of this phase per the brief.
- **Context:** Phase 14 ships Badge Templates with a generic shield silhouette as the inline graphic on every template. Long-term, issuers will want to upload their own graphic (PNG / SVG) so each template has a distinctive visual identity. The graphic should be content-addressed (uploaded to QS, referenced by URI from the template), respect visual sizing constraints (consistent rendering across small chip / list row / Detail Panel / expand-modal scales), and not require an image-processing pipeline.
- **Scope:**
  - File-upload flow on Badge Template create + new-version forms (drag-and-drop or file picker).
  - QS storage for the uploaded graphic; URI reference on the Badge Template artifact.
  - Renderer fallback to the shield silhouette when graphic is missing or unparsed.
  - Cross-tab / cross-role rendering of uploaded graphics on the Library list, right-panel detail, expand modal, Active Issuances chips (Phase 14.1+), and any future card / Detail Panel surfaces.
- **Out of scope:** Image moderation / approval workflows; graphic versioning beyond Badge Template versioning.

### 182. Amend Claim to include a PoE as an Asset
- **Status:** Open — High priority, deferred to a future phase
- **Effort:** M
- **Source:** Phase 14.2 (#169a) design huddle. Filed alongside the badge architectural correction so the OSHA / self-evaluation scenario surfaces a downstream need.
- **Context:** Today, a Claim references Assets via `claim.referencedAssetIds[]`. A self-PoE (Alice creates a PoE on her own self-Eval Result) lives separately on Alice's canvas and is disclosed to a third-party reviewer (e.g. OSHA) via a proof-of-eval DA — but it isn't bundled into the Claim's evidence list. A reviewer who wants to verify the Claim end-to-end currently has to either (a) run their own redundant evaluation, or (b) navigate from the Claim to the PoE separately. Allowing the Claim to reference a self-PoE as an Asset closes this loop: Full disclosure to the Claim now includes the PoE in scope.
- **Scope:**
  - Extend the AmendClaim flow's Asset picker to allow selecting PoEs the active actor owns.
  - Update the `referencedAssetIds[]` model (or introduce a parallel `referencedPoeIds[]`) to support PoE references.
  - Update view derivation + canvas adapter so the referenced PoE appears in the Claim's evidence column on the disclosure-grantee's canvas.
  - Update Run Evaluation's evidence picker to include referenced PoEs.
- **Out of scope:** Badge endorsement of referenced PoEs (that's still mediated through the parent Claim per Phase 14.2).

### 180. Substantive Output content for DA / EA / Parse Result expand modals
- **Status:** Open — defers to the Detail Panel cleanup phase (priority #5 on the locked queue)
- **Effort:** S–M
- **Priority:** Bundle with #58 / #104 / #116 / #161+ when that phase runs.
- **Source:** Phase 13.4 (#175) established the canonical `[Output] [JSON]` two-tab convention across every artifact's expand modal but only landed rich Output content for Eval Result and PoE. Asset / Claim's Output tabs reuse pre-13.4 content with the rename applied; DA / EA / Parse Result Output tabs render a thin scaffold (parties + subject + scope + terms / a small summary card / parsed-fields list).
- **Scope:**
  - Substantive Output content for DA expand modal — full disclosed-fields rendering in selective scope, full PoE-id list in proof-only scope, Asset id list in full scope (clickable rows).
  - Substantive Output content for EA expand modal — restrictions + incentives + acknowledgments rendered as cards, not a metadata table.
  - Substantive Output content for Parse Result expand modal — extracted fields with confidence chips at expanded scale + source Asset header.
  - Claim Output enriched — Referenced Assets list + Referenced Standards + Acknowledgments rendered inline rather than as count summaries.

### 174. Discriminated-union scope shape on proof-only DAs is a stepping stone
- **Status:** Open
- **Effort:** S
- **Priority:** Low (tech-debt note)
- **Source:** Phase 13.1 (#168a, bug fix). The Phase 13 grep-and-replace `evaluationResultIds → poeIds` over-migrated; Phase 13.1 restored the field name and made the proof-only DA factory a discriminated union with both fields existing on the data shape and `subject.kind` doing the discrimination.
- **Context:** Today's `scope.evaluationResultIds[]` (used when `subject.kind === 'evalResult'` for auto-disclosure DAs at Eval Result save time) and `scope.poeIds[]` (used when `subject.kind === 'poe'` for PoE-creation DAs and proof-only Claim DAs) carry the same conceptual payload — a list of "what's being disclosed". Maintaining two parallel fields is small but ongoing tax on every consumer that walks a DA's scope.
- **Proposed fix:** Unify under a single field. Two shapes considered: (a) `scope.targetIds: string[]` with `subject.kind` doing 100% of the discrimination (cleanest, requires touching every consumer); (b) keep both fields but add a typed accessor `getDisclosedIds(da)` that does the right thing based on `subject.kind` (less code change, more indirection).
- **Out of scope:** No user-visible behavior change. Pure data shape consolidation.

### 47. Real AI Shopper result streaming
- **Status:** Deferred to Phase 14
- **Effort:** L
- **Source:** Phase 7 placeholder implementation
- **Context:** The mock agent returns results in a single 2.2s batch. A real LLM-backed shopper would stream candidates as the search runs. Keep the split-screen pattern, but let rows appear one at a time with a short delay, each with a per-row confidence score that updates as more context is gathered. UI shape is already designed to absorb this — `results` array just needs incremental append instead of single assignment in `runMockSearch`.

### 121. Evaluate a Claim against multiple Requirements Sets simultaneously
- **Status:** Deferred to Phase 12
- **Effort:** L
- **Priority:** Low
- **Source:** Client planning discussion post-9C.
- **Context:** Currently Run Evaluation is 1:1 — one Claim, one Requirements Set, one Eval Result. Real evaluations often cover multiple standards (e.g., "does this part meet both MIL-STD-810 AND RoHS AND ITAR export-control?"). Extending the modal to accept N Requirements Sets would produce either N distinct Eval Results (one per set) or a single Eval Result that rolls up multi-set satisfaction.
- **Open design questions:**
  - Single Eval Result (multi-set) vs. multiple Eval Results (one per set)? The former is cleaner in the netgraph; the latter preserves per-set separability for partial supersede/amend.
  - If multi-set, how does the Eval Result Detail Panel present per-set breakdown?
  - Interaction with #117 (permissive re-run) — what if Bob originally evaluated against Set A, and now wants to expand to Set A + Set B?
- **Depends on:** Pairs with #106 (remove evidence picker — if evaluations are Claim-level, multi-Set-at-once is more tractable).

### 122. Remove evidence from a Claim despite prior evaluation (e.g., expired license)
- **Status:** Deferred to Phase 12
- **Effort:** M
- **Priority:** Low
- **Source:** Client planning discussion post-9C.
- **Context:** Today the platform locks evaluated Assets — they can't be removed from a Claim's scope once referenced by an Eval Result, to preserve the integrity of historical evaluations. Client's scenario: an Asset like an expired operating license becomes invalid over time. The Claim owner should be able to remove the expired Asset, which would mark prior Eval Results as needing re-evaluation.
- **Implications:**
  - Breaks a current platform invariant (evaluation locks on the Claim-owner side). Needs to decide the new invariant — probably "removal triggers supersede-required state on prior Eval Results."
  - Adds a new Eval Result status beyond ACTIVE / SUPERSEDED — perhaps STALE or REQUIRES_RE-EVAL.
  - Counterparty-side effect: Bob's old Eval Result on Alice's Claim is now marked STALE. Notification to Bob. His canvas might badge it. If he cares, he re-runs.
  - Interaction with transfer flows (#73): if an Asset backs a Claim that's been disclosed, removing it has amend-cascade implications.
- **Open design questions:** Scope carefully before implementing — this is a model-level change, not a UX addition.

### 123. "Reverse AI Shopper" — publish an Evaluation Agreement as an open RFP
- **Status:** Investigation
- **Effort:** L
- **Priority:** Low
- **Source:** Client planning discussion post-9C.
- **Context:** Flips the current AI Shopper model. Today, Bob (buyer) asks the platform "who can provide X?" and discovers sellers' published Claims. Proposed: Bob publishes an Evaluation Agreement as an RFP referencing specific Requirements Sets — "I'm looking for suppliers whose Claims can satisfy these requirements." Sellers discover the RFP, create Claims targeted at satisfying it (or point existing Claims at it), and engage the EA as grantees.
- **Implications:**
  - EAs become first-class discoverable objects in the Public Directory, not just downstream of disclosures.
  - Creates a new Claim-creation flow ("create a Claim targeting RFP X") — possibly auto-populates referenced Requirements Sets.
  - Shifts the directionality of the whole procurement model — sellers respond to buyer demand rather than buyers discovering seller supply.
  - Natural pair with #114 (umbrella disclosure) — a mature buyer with umbrella relationships could publish RFPs against their umbrella supplier pool without needing wide public visibility.
- **Open design questions:** Big one — does this change Radiant from "transparency infrastructure" to "procurement marketplace"? That's a positioning shift worth discussing with the client before scoping implementation.

### 129. Terminal-style scrambling-text erase during unravel
- **Status:** Open
- **Effort:** M
- **Priority:** Low
- **Source:** Phase 9D.2 scoping discussion — Andrew's deferred refinement to the unravel animation.
- **Context:** The Phase 9D.2 unravel currently fades content via a coordinated CSS keyframe — type label, name, owner row, and minibar all dissolve into transparency together. A more distinctive treatment Andrew flagged: replace the content fade with a scrambling-text "terminal erase" effect — characters flicker through a randomized glyph sequence (e.g., latin → katakana → bullets → empty) before each line clears, evoking a CRT terminal wipe. Reads as "data being scrubbed" rather than "card fading away."
- **Implementation sketch:** add a per-character text-scramble pass during the existing 600-1000ms unravel window. Could use a small RAF helper that mutates innerText on a target span every ~30ms with progressively-more-erased glyph pools. The text targets would be: type label first, name second, owner row third (each ~150-200ms staggered to match the existing Stage 3 spec). Need to be careful not to fight React's reconciliation — likely requires uncontrolled DOM access via ref during the animation window. The keyframe-driven opacity fade still runs underneath as the final clear.
- **Why it's deferred:** the current coordinated-fade unravel already meets the "card visibly leaves the canvas" bar set by the original spec. The scrambling text is a refinement that adds tonal character but doesn't change information conveyance. Worth picking up alongside other animation polish (#110 V2.1 marching ants restoration; #80 transfer accept reveal).
- **Depends on:** Phase 9D.2 unravel primitive (already shipped).

### 130. Asset hierarchy — squeeze child Assets into rows aligned with their parent
- **Status:** Open
- **Effort:** M
- **Priority:** Low
- **Source:** Phase 10.2 scoping — flagged as deferred polish.
- **Context:** Phase 10.2 places each owned Asset at `COL_OWN_ASSET + (depth × ASSET_COL_GAP)`. Within a depth group, vertical position uses the asset's index inside that group (root Assets stack `i*ROW_STEP`, depth-1 children also stack `i*ROW_STEP` within their column). Visual consequence: when you have a Sentinel-4 root and 3 children, the Sentinel-4 root sits at `y=0` and the three children sit at `y=0, 260, 520`. The children don't visually align with their parent — they spread vertically as if they were independent. Tighter UX would group children directly to the right of their parent, occupying rows adjacent to the parent rather than starting from `y=0` in the next column.
- **Implementation sketch:** instead of `i = peers.indexOf(asset)` for the within-depth row index, walk the hierarchy from roots and assign Y positions in tree-traversal order — each child cluster's first member sits at the parent's Y + vertical offset, subsequent siblings stack below. Roots without parents stack normally.

### 131. Asset dismissal flow with re-parenting + Claim-reference protection
- **Status:** Open
- **Effort:** L
- **Priority:** Medium
- **Source:** Phase 10.2 scoping — out-of-scope acknowledgement.
- **Context:** V2.2 has no Asset dismissal flow today — Assets are registered and persist. With Phase 10.2 hierarchy, dismissal raises new questions: (1) what happens to children of a dismissed Asset? Options: cascade-dismiss them, re-parent to grandparent, or re-parent to Actor (root). (2) What about Claims that reference the dismissed Asset? Cannot leave dangling references — either block dismissal or auto-revise Claims to drop the reference (requires user confirmation). (3) Effect on Parse Results / Eval Results derived from the Asset?
- **Design decisions needed:**
  - Cascade vs. re-parent semantics on dismissal — likely user choice in a confirmation modal.
  - Claim-reference protection — block dismissal vs. auto-revise Claim to drop the reference.
  - Audit trail — should dismissed Assets be retrievable later, or hard-deleted?

### 132. Umbrella DA edge visualization on directory layer
- **Status:** Deferred to Phase 14
- **Effort:** M
- **Priority:** Medium
- **Source:** Phase 11A scoping.
- **Context:** Phase 11A seeded a warm-path umbrella DA from ChipCo to GovCo. Phase 11B's cluster-click flow materializes a single Claim card on top of the cluster, but doesn't visualize the underlying umbrella DA itself — the relationship Bob has with ChipCo as a whole isn't drawn. A future phase could render an edge from the active actor's corner card to the cluster (or to its centroid), styled like the parent-layer DA edges (color + dash pattern by SDA type), making the umbrella relationship visible at a glance. Pairs with #114 (umbrella disclosure data model) — visualization should follow the schema work.

### 133. Passive Evaluation Agreement expiry notifications
- **Status:** Open
- **Effort:** S
- **Priority:** Low
- **Source:** Phase 11C scoping — flagged in the task brief as out-of-scope and filed as future polish.
- **Context:** Phase 11C added a demo-only `evaluationDeadline` check at Run Evaluation time — clicking Run Evaluation on an EA whose deadline is past blocks with a copy hint. There's no proactive surface for EA expiry: no "your EA expires in X days" notification, no "your EA expired Y days ago" follow-up. Production would handle this via platform eventing; the prototype could surface lightweight pre-expiry / post-expiry notifications on the grantee's inbox (`v22-ea-expiring-soon`, `v22-ea-expired`) for demo polish.
- **Proposed fix:** Periodic check (in-memory, on role load or render) over the active actor's EAs. Surface a `v22-ea-expiring-soon` notification at `deadline - 7 days` and a `v22-ea-expired` notification at `deadline + 0`. Click deep-links to the Claim. Both auto-dismiss when the user takes terminal action (e.g., requesting a fresh EA via the warm path).
- **Depends on:** Pairs with #115 (EA terms metadata schema).

### 163. Anchor Asset picker for EA-only requests on Directory Layer Claims
- **Status:** Deferred to Phase 14
- **Effort:** M
- **Source:** Phase 11E.1.6 QA — Andrew noticed the warm path's hardcoded anchor Asset.
- **Context:** When Bob requests an Evaluation Agreement on a Claim he discovered via the Radiant Network (Directory Layer), the resulting EA pulls the Claim onto Bob's canvas as a child of a hardcoded anchor Asset (currently Bob's Avionics Module). Bob should be able to choose which of his Assets to anchor against — the anchor Asset determines where the Claim materializes on his netgraph and which evidence the Claim's evaluation will reference.
- **Proposed fix:** Add an anchor-Asset picker step to `EARequestModal` when the request originates from a Directory Layer entry point. Picker defaults to no selection (per CLAUDE.md UX pattern: "Multi-select artifact pickers that require at least one selection… default to zero selected"). User explicitly chooses an Asset before continuing to the expiration + acknowledgments step.
- **Out of scope for this item:** Multi-anchor Claims (one Claim associated with multiple Assets on the requester's side) — future consideration if the model supports it.
- **Depends on:** Phase 14 Directory Layer work — pairs naturally with #43 (Clickable Directory Layer dots) and #46 (Corner-node morph on Directory entry/exit) since those define the entry-point UX.

### 162. EA revocation copy + Directory Layer post-revocation visualization
- **Status:** Deferred to Phase 14
- **Effort:** S (copy) + part of #132 (visualization)
- **Source:** Phase 11E.1.3 QA — Andrew's architectural note on revocation semantics.
- **Context:** When a grantor revokes an Evaluation Agreement, the affected Claim node is removed from both parties' netgraphs (the grantor's immediately; the grantee's after notification → Detail Panel notice → Dismiss). However, the underlying Disclosure Agreement persists — the grantee retains visibility into the Claim's Assets / fields / Eval Result per the DA's disclosure type, just no longer pulled onto their canvas. Today this creates a "where did it go" UX gap on the grantee side: the DA is still active but neither party's netgraph reflects the relationship visually.
- **Architectural resolution:** The DA-only relationship surfaces on the Radiant Network (Directory Layer). The grantee's corner card carries a disclosure edge to the grantor's cluster (paralleling #132 Umbrella DA edge visualization), styled by DA type. The Claim itself remains visible as a dot in the grantor's cluster via the Public Directory mechanism when the Claim has a published listing. The "still have access via DA" relationship lives in the Directory rather than on the netgraph.
- **Proposed fix (two parts):**
  1. **Copy update (Phase 14, alongside #132):** EA revocation flow's grantee-side notice gains a line referencing where the DA lives post-revocation, e.g. "Your Disclosure Agreement to this Claim remains active. View it in the Radiant Network." Same on the grantee-side Dismiss confirmation.
  2. **Visualization (already scoped under #132):** Render the DA-only edge from grantee's corner card to grantor's cluster on the Directory Layer.
- **Depends on:** Pairs with #132 (Umbrella DA edge visualization) and #43 (Clickable Directory Layer dots). Both are Phase 14.

### 114. Umbrella Disclosure concept + second seller role with pre-existing DA to Bob
- **Status:** Partial (Phase 11A seeded ChipCo + warm-path DA; full umbrella semantics + Asset-hierarchy interaction still scoping)
- **Effort:** M
- **Priority:** High (enables #113's two-flow contrast — already shipped in 11C as the warm-path EA-only flow)
- **Source:** Andrew planning conversation post-9C — modeling realistic procurement relationships.
- **Context:** Real procurement doesn't ad-hoc request disclosure on every transaction. Mature buyer-supplier relationships have framework agreements covering visibility broadly, with specific actions exercised under those frameworks. The platform supports this via "umbrella" Disclosure Agreements at the Radiant Network layer — a standing DA from a seller to a buyer covering the seller's entire published catalog. Set up administratively (out-of-app, like a master service agreement at onboarding); not a UX flow within Radiant. Phase 11A added Dave/ChipCo as the second seller actor with a pre-existing DA to Bob; Phase 11C exercised the warm-path EA flow against it.
- **Removed Phase 12.2** — out of scope; umbrella disclosure ability deferred to a future Admin App, separate from this interface. Phase 11A's Dave/ChipCo seed data + warm-path EA flow remains in place to support narrative needs.

### 6. Selected-edge state persistence through layer changes
- **Status:** ✅ Verified — no fix needed (Phase 20.2, 2026-05-27). The Phase 3 concern was "selecting an edge then triggering a dive/surface resets the highlight because material mutation doesn't survive the mesh rebuild." Verified in V2.2: edge selection does NOT persist through dive/surface, but this is **intentional and correct**, not the feared material-rebuild bug. Both `handleDive` (V2Canvas ~1655) and `handleSurface` (~1859) call `onCloseSel?.()` → V2App `handleCloseSel`, which clears `selectedEdgeId` + `edgeMenu` (citing spec §4.4 node/edge mutual exclusivity). The `onCloseSel` on dive is REQUIRED — it's what closes the Asset Detail Panel for the Phase 20.0/20.1 single-panel dive semantics; on surface it prevents stale child-layer selections from ghosting onto the parent. The prompt's prescribed outcome-(C) fix ("remove the clear from the dive path") would regress the shipped single-panel dive behavior, so it was deliberately NOT applied. The material-rebuild concern is moot because selection is cleared at the state level upstream. Closing as won't-fix / superseded by the `onCloseSel`-clear architecture. (A future "re-select the parent edge on surface" continuity enhancement would be new scope, not a bug fix.)

### 200. Directory mini-card swap at higher zoom
- **Status:** ✅ Complete (Phase 16.2.7, 2026-05-17). Shipped via the LOD-card-swap brief: `MAX_ZOOM` lifted 1.5 → 5.0; two thresholds (`MID_LOD_THRESHOLD = MINI_CARD_W / DOT_GRID = 3.333…`, `LOD_THRESHOLD = CARD_W / DOT_GRID = 4.375`); above MID_LOD Claim dots replaced by `AssetNodeMini` HTML overlays; above LOD they swap to full `AssetNode`. RFP markers stay as hollow squares throughout (RFP card swap deferred to a future phase paired with RFP Detail Panels). Cluster pillbox labels stay as `PillboxLabel`. Cards render at FIXED NATURAL PIXEL SIZE (no `transform: scale(${zoom})`) — at the threshold, screen-space inter-dot spacing exactly equals card width, so cards never overlap horizontally. Viewport-cull skips cards outside `viewport + cardW/cardH` buffer on each edge. Dot mesh hidden via `dotsMesh.visible = zoom < MID_LOD_THRESHOLD` `useEffect`. Hover cleared on LOD entry (raycast against hidden mesh would yield no hits). `pinned` survives LOD transitions so the relevant card / dot renders `isSelected={true}`. New `onCardClick` `useCallback` mirrors dot click flow (sets `pinned`, calls `onClaimDotClick`) but skips the animated pan/zoom — user clicked a card they can already see. `AssetNode.jsx` NOT modified — brief's hard rule preserved.
